The issue of women in foreign policy, as I believe, is an important one, particularly on the basis of UN resolution 1325, which concerns the contribution of women to peace-making work and peace work as a whole. Therefore, we have also been particularly committed to this issue in accordance with the legislation in Austria with the Development Cooperation Act. I regularly meet women's groups on my travel abroad in order to also get a better insight into the overall problem. However, I have always set special accents here, for example with the Middle East Women's Conference last May or with the conference for Serbian and Kosova women last autumn – something that was an important contribution to understanding civil society in the face of the tense emotional and political situation in the region. I will continue these contributions. We are also committed, in particular, to the EU and UN missions, to ensuring that the points of view, the concerns of women, but also the protection of women and the protection of children in crisis regions. I would like to pay particular attention to this. During our EU Presidency and after that, we have made sure that appropriate directives are drawn up especially in the case of EU missions, wherever we can influence them. This has clearly been an Austrian contribution to this work.

I cannot answer what was now the German decision-making basis in detail. But I think that if I see the commitment in other regions and areas, such as Afghanistan, it has to do with it. The Austrian exports have overall doubled since our accession to the EU. This must be borne in mind: In the meantime, our export economy earns €10. I would also like to briefly address the issue of employment: In 2007 alone, 63 000 jobs were created, 59 per cent of them almost 60 per cent of them in the export sector. That is, our membership in the European Union, especially since enlargement with our neighbours in the East and the South, has paid for Austria.

The Eighth Environmental Control Report is really a very exemplary report, both in terms of the width of the topics dealt with and in terms of their depth and clarity. I consider the separation in printed paper on the one hand and further electronic evidence on the other, to be very, very valuable, as well as the recommendations at the end of each chapter. They provide a good overview of action options. In this sense, I would really like to thank all those who contribute to this excellent work on behalf of my group and, I think, on behalf of the whole House. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP and Greens.]] It is not always that we can have real joy with the figures you deliver, because it is not always the most beautiful numbers, not the ones we would like, but I think it's certainly scientifically very waterproof and very well-founded numbers that provide a very important basis for political decisions. In view of the fullness of the report, I would also like to pick out only one area that seems very important to me, that is the question of air. We have some legal instruments at our disposal, starting from the Emissions Maximum Volumes Act Air, the Ozone Act and the IG Air, to a whole range of regulations in this area, which are embedded in a very complex environmental matter from the Industrial Code to plant law. At all these screws and girls we can turn, all of which we can change by 6. To meet the European Union's environmental action programme, i.e. to reduce the burden of air pollutants and to see that there are no significant negative effects on human health or the environment. The challenge, as well as what is now a seasonal issue, which is very moving, which unfortunately many people fear rightly, is certainly the fight against dust dust. It doesn't work locally, as we know. It doesn't even work regionally. We must put this important task on many different shoulders. On this issue, we must coordinate highly intelligently and distribute the skills meaningfully. There are, we know, different sources of fine dust, there are variations that are regional, sectoral, meteorological and temporal, and the Federal Environment Agency tells us that even if existing action catalogues and the fine dust programmes already available were implemented in full, we would not achieve the target values that are actually prescribed by the IG air and which we would have to implement under the EU directive on air quality and clean air. That means that there is a need for more initiative, and we really need to use all possibilities and turn on many wheels to reduce fine dust emissions. I would like to mention a few, there are infinitely many, but a few that seem particularly important to me: It is necessary that we coordinate the rules on investment permits with the existing state of the art. We need federal regulations for dust limits for all industries, including those which are not yet covered, such as lime, chipboard and fertilizer production. It also seems important to me that we reach relatively close to the technical potential – in some areas there are still rules that are very, very lax, although with the state of the art an imposure of one fifth would be attainable. It will be easier to apply measures to existing old plants. That's not always easy. There are many obstacles and obstacles on different levels. I think it is equally important to introduce a particle filter requirement for off-road equipment and vehicles and to remove the various derogations, which are extremely bulky and complicated and difficult to apply in execution. As far as off-road vehicles are concerned, there should also be a pickerel for these vehicles. They should also be subject to a technical and therefore air-related review, as we must do in the case of cars. And it is also important to finally search through the many exceptions to the bans on trucks and to change something to make it clearer. I think we should also use the instrument of environmental promotion abroad. We know that fine dust is being supplied here from a very far distance, so that we cannot cope with measures in Austria alone. We really need to see that we also do something in the surrounding foreign country! It would be important for me to have an annual review of the passenger cars in order to ensure that existing limit values can be met in the exhaust gases. It has to be a uniform national marking of motor vehicles – and especially of trucks! – give categories such as fine dust or NOx because, for example, an emission-dependent toll will be possible, which gave us a lot. There must be ecological, but also economically sound criteria for the approval of new installations in heavily burdened areas: Just not being able to build anything, that is not a solution, there must be some kind of compensation between existing and new plants! We want a mandatory diesel particle duty for new vehicles as well as promotion models for diesel lcatalysts. In addition, there is a truly wonderful 2005 Environment Ministry option paper, which unfortunately has been on ice since then and is frozen. I think we should defy it, extract it, reforest it and implement all that makes sense. It would really be a big concern to me to do something together, especially in the IG air, but also in all other relevant laws. All Austrians have the right to breathe clean air, and here we have the power to take part in the framework conditions for creating clean air.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-01-31-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00047_d8e13312	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The present environmental control report contains in its respective sections extensive recommendations, which the basic to be a key factor in the further development of Austrian environmental policy. Many recommendations have already been made because we want to take account of the needs of today's generation without endangering the opportunities of future generations. I would like to dedicate a few points to waste management. Ladies and gentlemen, waste management is the whole of the activities and tasks associated with the prevention, reduction, recovery and disposal of waste. The amount of waste in Austria increased from 49 million tonnes to 54 million tonnes from 1999 to 2004, while hazardous waste with 1 million tonnes – one has to say thank God – remains constant. Waste treatment and recycling reduced the proportion of untreated landfill waste from 1999 to 2004 from 28.5 to 7.7 percent. Since the mid-1990s, the volume of waste has grown as much as GDP and the increase in household waste has been significantly stronger. Waste prevention, ladies and gentlemen, must be a criterion of product design in the production process and in environmentally conscious consumption behaviour. Every citizen and every citizen is invited to separate the trash as soon as they throw it away. Waste separation is starting in every household, and it is gratifying that the population is ready to do so. Much is already happening. The following follow-up: What happens to the separate waste? – Here the waste combustion plants are a big, yes, partly feared topic. The Environmental Control Report proposes that these must be equipped with modern smoke gas cleaning and that modern technology should also prevent the distribution of pollutants in the environment in mechanical and biological waste treatment. Here the population needs to be more informed in order to take the fear of these plants. The example in my federal state, in southern Burgenland, in Heiligenkreuz, shows this. Finally, ladies and gentlemen: The environmental report is well prepared and very comprehensive. Now it is about implementation. For the purposes of the Treibhaus reduction targets, appropriate techniques should be used to protect resources, to protect the raw materials, or to use them effectively. I would also like to thank the officials for preparing this detailed and clear report.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-01-31-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00047_d8e14188	Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! Unfortunately, I had to accompany some friends from Parliament, so my short delay. [[Cheerfulness and applause with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] The six Austrian national parks extend from the Pannonian low plain to the Hohe Tauern. They are habitat for many animal species, recreation areas for people, but also economic areas, and this is particularly important for the people living there. In the last quarter of the century, since the founding of these national parks, much has been achieved, especially as we have heard today, international recognition. It is also important for me that the initial fear of the property owners that they are restricted in their management could be scattered. But if you are dealing with the tourist development of the National Park municipalities in Austria, it quickly comes to the point that in almost all 60 National Park municipalities, the number of summer stays has declined dramatically. This is particularly dramatic in Eastern Europe, where up to 30 percent and more declines are due in part. This is all the more annoyed than the public hand, federal government and countries, a total of about €25 million annually flows into these areas. At present, however, all public money flows into system maintenance, in some overcrowded hunting leases, in the construction of larch shields. In order to dispute this immediately: In principle, I think the protection and protection of special areas is of course a great thing. The farmers who have to operate in these areas have also been granted all the revenue. It is also a question of the fact that we will continue to comply with the agreements in this context. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is important, however, that the national parks serve not only nature, protection of nature, but also the rest of the local population, to the benefit – and the experience shows that the national park idea needs new impulses. It is not the best national park on whose edge tourism dies the fastest. At present, the national parks are too much an insider tip for trained alpineists, “normal” people, especially older, families with small children, hardly get to enjoy the national parks. It would be extremely important to show the parks, because you can only protect and love what you know. So we need our own marketing professionals at eye level – especially important – with the local scientific directors, with their own budget that marketing can operate. The marketing idea must become a central task of the National Park administration. We need active product development. Which products are currently possible, which can be redesigned with some good will, which would be useful and legally and financially possible at present? We need answers to these questions. One should also have the courage to think of legal changes. If you look at the structure of the national parks and the responsibilities, you can quickly expect that we have three national national parks in the Hohe Tauern National Park, for example, but for this the marketing is charged to local tourism associations that have scarce budgets due to their own economic weaknesses. Those who recognize themselves in the Hohe Tauern National Parks know that two Federal Ministers, three Land Councils for Nature Conservation and three Land Councils are responsible for tourism. When the National Park Manager Dipl.-Ing. Stotter in his report says – I quote –: “Tourism is a touristist” then it is evidenced by an attitude that escapes from the tourist responsibility and shifts it to the tourist associations, but which have no influence on the national park and the product of the national park. I therefore consider it extremely important that we also consider new structures in responsibility and not just more money. The aim of our application is therefore that the competent Federal Minister for Agriculture, Forestry, Environment and Water Management and the Federal Minister for Labour and Economic Affairs should set up an interministerial working group involving Austria's advertising and the federal states, which is concerned about the analysis of the tourist brand presence of the Austrian National Park and the National Park regions, and which is based on these proposals for improvement. I ask you to help the people living in the National Park so that what Vice-chanc Molterer, head of the Burgstaller State, also the national councils who were present, said last September at the initiation of the National Park Centre in Mittersill: Not only protect, but above all also benefit – and this for all economic forms! Thank you.

[[General condolences.]] But now on the subject, ladies and gentlemen. – Before I come to the National Parks, I would like to give an overview of the 2007 tourism year, because I think it is important to note the importance of tourism for the economic and working location of Austria, and tell you how successful last year was, because we can be very proud of the tourism sector. There are excellent successes to be recorded here! In Austria last year, we had 121 million receipts and thus, in 2007, for the first time since 1994 again strong growth compared to the previous year. Overall, we had excellent increases at the arrivals and could even exceed a record mark here: Last year we had 31.1 million arrivals, which is really a great success for our tourism industry. There have been increases in all federal states. I think it is nice to say that there has been very great growth in all the federal states. We see what has been happening for years, a clear trend towards quality tourism. The four-, five-star categories have significant increases; in the three-star range we have increases of just over 1 percent. Worse is the situation in the single and two-star category and in the private quarters, where decreases are recorded. That is to say, our strategy, which for years has been to finance upgrades to promote appropriate quality improvements, has proved to be correct, and we will continue to do so. There was a brilliant start in the winter season 2007/08 and in November/December I could not believe first myself when I saw it – the WIFO has calculated a preliminary figure. We are talking here, ladies and gentlemen, of a turnover of 3.1 billion euros alone in these two months! This is, I believe, really something to emphasize, because it shows once again the great success and competitiveness of Austrian tourism companies. Of course, we were able to benefit accordingly through the great snow situation in the tourism companies, and we will see how the winter season as a whole goes out. We have a relatively early Easter date this year, the Easter holidays will be very early, but the booking situation here leaves us very optimistic. It is very important for me, as the Secretary of State for Labour, to see tourism as a work engine. We also have excellent figures here: Nearly 169 000 employees are employed in the tourism industry. And this, ladies and gentlemen, is 5.1 percent of all employees in Austria. Women, too, offer excellent opportunities to tourism and the tourism industry. That is why we created a tourism platform between the federal government and countries and held a first meeting in the BMWA in October in order to simply force the networking between the federal government and countries much more strongly, with the national tourism directors who are here very strong in the boat and really work together at an excellent level. Both, federal government and countries, carry this tourism platform, where it is a matter of working together to develop strategies for the sustainable security of the Austrian tourist site and to promote and continue the competitiveness of Austrian tourism companies accordingly. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the SPÖ.]] An important aspect in this year is, of course, the EURO 2008. It will be an important factor in the area of tourism advertising through Austria. The Austrian advertising has included this in their new campaign, there are own subjects. This is simply about using the EURO itself, not to apply for the football event itself, but to use the attention that is here aimed at Austria worldwide and to position Austria as a beautiful holiday country, as a holiday destination. There are a total of 650 activities in over 30 markets in this context. Here Austria's advertising has been successfully and accordingly active. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] On this motion for a resolution on the national parks, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to say in principle that we naturally have to deal with a very sensitive matter in the national parks, because it is important to take into account the area of protection of nature, which is a priority in the national parks, and therefore automatically restricts the potential of tourist use and the way in which tourism concepts are implemented here is sensitive and difficult. This is about the protected landscape – and this has the first priority – and of course about the economic aspect for the communities in these areas. In the present proposal, our House and the Ministry of Life are called upon to set up an interministerial task force to develop a holistic In this context, Austria's advertising has an important role to play in the marketing of national parks. To this end, it should be said in principle: We have set the cornerstones for setting up nature parks with Article 15a contracts with the countries. The responsibility on the federal side lies here – and you know this – primarily with the Ministry of Life. The national laws and regulations will then be adopted by the countries. So the first responsibility lies with the countries when it comes to the national parks. The problem is that in the six national parks in Austria, we have very different orientations, which are widely marketable. And the agreement of the individual tourist communities and countries – there are often several federal states in a national park; in the Hohe Tauern it is even three – is differently well developed, the cooperation works differently well. This, of course, makes the uniform – or not uniform – appearance of a national park very different, and this makes marketing difficult accordingly. The Österreich Werbung association can only apply for marketable products around the world. This is even the first principle in marketing, in advertising: If there is no product, you can't apply! – This is exactly the problem that we have in this case. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, the Austrian advertising does not create any regional marketing plans and is not a sales organisation either. That means that this must first be developed by the competent countries. The Ministry of Life is currently working very intensively on a 25-year strategy for the Austrian National Parks, where it is of course a question of anchoring an appropriate tourism relationship and also of taking this into account. Here the BMWA is very happy to provide expertise with the experts and to discuss it here. It is easy to understand in principle the extent to which tourist use in the national parks is still possible, especially with regard to nature conservation. Here we are very happy, as I have already said, and on the part of Austria we are very happy to provide advertising here – I have already said in the Committee – to an expert who has already been made known and who is available with his know-how accordingly. The Austrian advertising is already in the process of applying the national parks in their application for the “Marke Österreich”, where nature is a very, very important asset. Mr Hauser, you have shown a whole package of products, but at the same time this shows the problem: This is a whole package, and these are only conditionally uniform strategies that are going through like a red thread. And that's exactly what makes it so difficult. – Here we are happy with our overall expertise. I think it is the wrong order that Mr Bucher and Mr Hauser have seen here: First, the money, and then we'll see what we're doing with it! – From our point of view, it must be easy to say: First, consider the product and then make the strategy available accordingly from the financial framework. The Court of Auditors has dealt closely with the topic of close-up suppliers and shopping centres, particularly in the Länder of Salzburg, Carinthia, Styria and Upper Austria. The Schwammerls often shoot unmistakable specialist markets and shopping centres out of the ground, often without the appropriate transport concepts and often without a previous qualified review of the impact on local suppliers in the surrounding communities or even their own local community. Although the Court of Auditors identifies individual activities in the direction of protecting and safeguarding local suppliers, the legal design and steering possibilities of the respective national governments are not or clearly not used in the direction of the new challenges. In Upper Austria, this situation has already been taken into account in 2005 in the innovation of the spatial planning system, at least in recognizable approaches, and proposals from the Court of Auditors – such as no settlement of motorway shopping centres – have been included in the law. Nevertheless, there are in the central area shopping stamps such as PlusCity, UNO Shopping, Haid Center et cetera, which cause enormous concerns to traffic planners and the travelers and counterparts in part enormous trouble. In this context, the Court of Auditors recommends a transport connection charge or a steering charge by taxation of shopping centres and the promotion of local services by such means. An intercommunal financial compensation is also being discussed again and again. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, cities, communities and the federal states are called upon to think intensively in the sense of consumers, of the commuters, but also of the close-up carers and workers. The Federal Government could make valuable contributions to uniform arrangements, for example, in the context of the constitutional discussion. A first example of the rejection of such a shopping centre in Upper Austria has been the planned “shopping village place” in the Innkreis since last year. The competent State Council of Sigl did not give an approval for the construction after the spatial impact assessment, as there was a fear of a massive displacement competition in this region and an irresistible volume of transport. This decision is also in line with the Court of Auditors' recommendation that permits for shopping centres over 1,500 square metres of sales space should be increased in central places, such as district cities. In addition, according to my information for each district, a regional local delivery concept will be developed in Upper Austria. These are important steps in the right direction. I would like to thank the Court of Auditors for their valuable contributions.

I once made a proposal on the basis of the local tax in the Committee. We made the first attempt at the intercommunal financial compensation in 2003. We talked to the mayors of the communities who said: That's a great thing! There will be tax revenue It is distributed intercommunally, and each municipality has its share of it. At least in Carinthia, I don't know a model of that kind that would have really worked. That's what I'm saying quite openly. I believe that in Upper Austria – and you have presented it, Mr President – this works there or there. My proposal would have been to consider taking the municipal tax as an instrument, the municipal tax, which is about to be redistributed, into the hands of the countries and to distribute it there either to municipalities or to economic areas. In fact, it is true that the communities are giving each other a hard fight for the settlement of farms, some of them of course have the geographical advantage and can also play the part and many marginal communities are left, which do not at all benefit from it. Sometimes it is also these communities that have the largest road and road network to be preserved, and these are ultimately the departure communities. The most fair solution would be to find a model to ensure that these communities also have a functional budget, a budget that will enable them to fulfil their tasks. This would be a proposal on how we can maintain rural areas – in the interests of agriculture, in the interests of the population living there and ultimately in the interest of tourism.

First topic in your debate on the Court of Auditors reports today was refugee care. I would also like to point out that the audited administrative reform project, the support processes, has achieved enormous cost savings after recommendations from the Court of Auditors have been implemented. Processes have been optimised, controlling has been strengthened, project execution has been improved. At the end of the examination, the benchmarks determined by the Court of Auditors have been incorporated into the revision of the spatial planning law in Salzburg. I quote at this point the competent Council of State from Salzburg: I wasn't happy at all at the beginning about the audit and I thought what this is about the Court of Auditors. In retrospect, the work of the Court of Auditors is very positive, and I can take some positive action from it. - Quote. That is to say, it is also apparent that the function of the Court of Auditors as the Federal State institutions will also be taken up positively by the other local authorities and will then draw their lessons from it. The reports on teacher personnel planning or school supervision, which have just been discussed, also show that there are competence-related problems in this area, which is why the legislator is also required. We have a division of competence in this area, because the responsibility for expenditure and financing falls apart. If you look at teacher staff planning and school supervision, you can see: It is precisely the divided distribution of competence that leads to the fact that continuous and longer-term target-oriented work is not possible. In this area, for example, we have federal bodies, district school inspectors, national school inspectors – Mr Neugebauer knows that – who cannot carry out their tasks, because they lack both the organisational and personnel development documents, but also the management of the human resources deployment. It is possible because the federal government pays the federal bodies acting in countries, but in some countries the authority remains with the institutions of the countries. This division of competence in some countries does not allow for an overall view. The final report concerns the preparatory courses we have examined at the music universities, namely at the universities of music and performing art in Vienna and Graz as well as at Mozarteum in Salzburg. Here, too, it has been shown that huge problems were there, that the objectives set were not achieved, that a high financial effort was faced with a lower benefit or a lower success. But I would also like to say to honourable Members that 90 percent of the Court's recommendations have been implemented in this area. In other words, the Court of Auditors has shown that its recommendations have content that can be implemented. They were taken over and implemented by the audited bodies. This is certainly a good job for the taxpayer, but also a proof for you that control works. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-01-31-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00047_d8e25455	Madam President. High house! The compensation tax for disabled people who are not employed in businesses has been a topic here in the House for many years. However, what hurts me particularly is that the public hand does not fulfil its obligation and employs a beneficiary disabled per 25 employees. In this respect, a lot is required of the private sector, but the public sector does not comply with this obligation, with the public hand paying this compensation tax, because the money is actually turning around in the circle. It would therefore be good if we were to make sure a public service obligation that a beneficiary disabled person is employed per 25 employees. What I would like to address on this issue is the fact that many entrepreneurs are afraid to hire a favored disabled person because there are major problems in the area of termination. I've also talked to the disabled, who say: I do not say that I have a degree of disability, otherwise the businessman does not take me, and I have problems at work. Come on! There are many disabilities, which are not quite obvious, and these disabled people often do not say so either because they are afraid not to get the job. I would like to present today a proposal on a progressive compensatory tax. Mrs Haidlmayr has often proposed raising the compensatory tax in order to increase the incentive for entrepreneurs. I would like to propose a progressive compensation tax so that not small businesses are taxed for a fee, because for a small company with a very specific staff structure, it may be more difficult to hire a disabled person than for a farm which has 200, 300, 400 employees. This operation can be much easier to fulfil due to the logistical possibilities in personnel planning. My proposal now looks like a regulation for the Minister of Social Affairs to set a basic amount for the first disadvantaged people who are not employed in the company. And for any other disadvantaged disabled people who are not recruited, the compensation tax increases by 50 percent each. Then there's a cover, because that can't go into the infinite. That is why there is, of course, a cover, a grinding system. That means in concrete terms: If you don't hire a beneficiary disabled person, you pay a compensation tax of 209 €. In the second place, that would make up as much as €31.50. This goes up to a maximum amount. So if you have a company that has 273 employees, and you say: I don't employ any favored disabled persons at all!, then you would pay a total of €2 090 so far. According to my proposal, this would cost this company much more, namely €6 700. I know that's an incision. But I believe that we can give this incentive, because it is easier for larger companies to meet their obligations than small businesses. For businesses with 60, 74 employees, nothing changes at all, as everything remains with the old. But it will be more expensive for the larger farms if they fail to comply with this obligation. I would ask you to discuss this request in committee. I know that it will not be implemented one too one, but I would ask that this idea be followed up by the progressive compensatory tax.
